Fantasy football Start ‘Em, Sit ‘Em: 30 players to start or sit in Week 9

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Week 9 of the 2026 NFL season has already brought with it plenty of chaos, as several teams are making notable moves at the quarterback position.

A microcosm of the league's greater movement can be seen with the Baltimore Ravens. The Ravens lost Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley to long-term injuries and will start undrafted rookie Joe Fagnano on "Thursday Night Football." However, they are also planning to trade for Arizona Cardinals quarterback Jacoby Brissett over the weekend, so their quarterback room is in flux.

The same is true with the Seattle Seahawks, where Drew Lock will replace an injured Sam Darnold, and with the Cleveland Browns, where Shedeur Sanders may start with Deshaun Watson in concussion protocol. And that's not to mention the Philadelphia Eagles' decision to bench Tanner McKee to see what they have in the potential-packed Joe Milton.

That swath of quarterback changes will leave many fantasy managers with difficult start 'em, sit 'em decisions. Those quandaries will also exist at other positions like running back, where fantasy footballers will have to decide whether to trust Ashton Jeanty or Tyler Allgeier, among others.

Who can you start and sit in fantasy football for Week 9 of the 2026 NFL season? USA TODAY Sports outlines 30 players to start or sit.

Malik Willis, Miami Dolphins (vs. Detroit Lions)

Willis is starting to round into form for the Dolphins and has scored multiple touchdowns in three consecutive games. During that span, he has averaged 37.7 rushing yards per game, which will raise his floor in most matchups. The Lions have allowed the most fantasy points per game (FPPG) to quarterbacks this season, so Willis should be positioned to have a strong showing in what figures to be a favorable, high-scoring matchup.

Drew Lock, Seattle Seahawks (vs. Arizona Cardinals)

The Cardinals are one of just three NFL teams to allow 18 passing touchdowns to quarterbacks this season, along with the Lions and the Las Vegas Raiders. Unsurprisingly, Arizona has allowed a league-high 29.8 fantasy points per game to the position because of that, so Lock should be positioned for a nice outing in which he could toss multiple touchdowns.

Joe Milton, Philadelphia Eagles (vs. New York Giants)

If you're looking for a fun dart throw, Milton could be worth considering. He's set to start in place of Tanner McKee and has the cannon arm and elite athleticism needed to blossom into a fantasy asset. The Giants have allowed the seventh-most FPPG to quarterbacks, so Milton has a chance to emerge as a decent streamer.

Quinshon Judkins, Cleveland Browns (at New Orleans Saints)

Judkins has been boom-or-bust as a touchdown-dependent fantasy option, but he should be poised for a good outing against the Saints. New Orleans has allowed the most FPPG to running backs this season, including a league-high 11 rushing touchdowns to the position. With that in mind, Judkins should be able to find his way into the end zone.

Jonah Coleman, Denver Broncos (at Carolina Panthers)

Coleman took on a lion's share of Denver's backfield touches in Week 9, posting 104 total yards and a touchdown on 15 touches (11 carries, four receptions). The rookie should continue in that role and has a great matchup against a Panthers defense that has allowed the second-most FPPG to running backs this season.

Tyler Allgeier, Arizona Cardinals (at Seattle Seahawks)

Seattle is a tough matchup for any running back, but Allgeier figures to get great volume with Jeremiyah Love (ankle) on IR and Bam Knight dealing with a concussion. That could be enough to make him a touchdown-dependent flex play after he popped off for 191 total yards and two touchdowns against the Cowboys.

Jadarian Price, Seattle Seahawks (vs. Arizona Cardinals)

Price and Zach Charbonnet split carries for the Seahawks against the Bears. Neither was particularly effective but each gets a much easier matchup in Week 9 against a Cardinals defense that has been middle-of-the-pack against running backs this season. Price has some explosiveness, so don't be surprised if this is his breakout game.

Caleb Douglas, Miami Dolphins (vs. Detroit Lions)

As mentioned, the Lions have been weak against the pass this year. If we're recommending to start Willis, then Douglas should get a nod too, as the rookie has notched six receptions for 113 yards and two touchdowns in two games since returning from an early-season ankle injury.

Ladd McConkey, Los Angeles Chargers (vs. Houston Texans)

Trusting McConkey may seem risky, given that Justin Herbert has struggled under pressure this season. That said, the Texans are allowing the second-most FPPG to receivers this season and McConkey is fresh off a four-catch, 86-yard outing that was one of his best of the season. He should be in line for a solid showing against the Texans.

Josh Downs, Indianapolis Colts (vs. Dallas Cowboys)

Could this be a showcase game for Downs ahead of the 2026 NFL trade deadline? It sure feels like it. Dallas has allowed the fifth-most FPPG to receivers this season, and the Colts may be eager to boost Downs' trade value by targeting him early and often in this spot.

Brian Thomas Jr., Jacksonville Jaguars (at Baltimore Ravens)

The same is true of Thomas, who could be traded as he sees his role shrink in Jacksonville. He caught a touchdown last week and could be in line for ample playing time if the Jaguars can build a big lead against a Joe Fagnano-led Ravens team on "Thursday Night Football."

Mike Gesicki, Cincinnati Bengals (at Atlanta Falcons)

Gesicki has scored in five consecutive games and six of the seven he has played this season. Joe Burrow has thrown 13 touchdown passes over his last three games, so Gesicki should continue to have high touchdown upside regardless of his opponent.

Greg Dulcich, Miami Dolphins (vs. Detroit Lions)

Dulcich has posted at least four catches and 37 yards in four of his last five games. Now, he gets to face a Lions defense that is allowing the most receptions (61), receiving yards (684) and receiving touchdowns (11) to tight ends this season. He could emerge as a surprise top-10 tight end this week.

Jacksonville Jaguars (at Baltimore Ravens)

The Jaguars are playing the Ravens on a short week. Baltimore is expected to start third-string quarterback Joe Fagnano, an undrafted rookie, after injuries to Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley. Fagnano completed 10 of 21 passes for 91 yards and an interception while being sacked three times against the Bills. The Jaguars should pressure him into numerous mistakes while their top-ranked run defense should be able to bottle up Derrick Henry.

The Lions will likely give up some yardage and points to the Dolphins, but they should also generate plenty of sacks. Detroit has 24 sacks this season, third-most in the NFL, while Malik Willis has been sacked 21 times through seven starts.

Bryce Young, Carolina Panthers (vs. Denver Broncos)

Young has regressed over the last two weeks after a torrid start to the season, completing just 54.4% of his passes for 372 yards, two touchdowns and three interceptions during Carolina's two-game losing skid. The main issue? Young has been pressured a lot in those contests, and he has just a 64 passer rating when pressured compared to 106.3 when kept clean, per Pro Football Focus. The Broncos rank fourth in pressure rate league-wide, per the NFL's Next Gen Stats, so they should be able to limit Young in this favorable spot.

Bo Nix, Denver Broncos (at Carolina Panthers)

The Panthers have a banged-up secondary, but it still won't be easy to justify starting Nix. The Broncos quarterback has looked better recently, throwing for exactly 222 yards in back-to-back games and scoring twice in each. Still, the Panthers are typically more susceptible against the run, so Nix has a lower ceiling than most streaming options this week.

Daniel Jones, Indianapolis Colts (vs. Dallas Cowboys)

Normally, the quarterback playing the Cowboys is a solid streaming option. However, with Jones facing down the potential for a benching and having failed to throw multiple touchdown passes in a game this season, it's best to steer clear of him.

Ashton Jeanty, Las Vegas Raiders (at San Francisco 49ers)

Can you really trust Jeanty after the Raiders gave him just two carries against the Jets while giving Mike Washington Jr. a whopping 25? Jeanty will remain productive as a receiver, but his uncertain between-the-tackles workload makes it hard to trust the former first-round pick, especially against a 49ers defense allowing just 3.6 yards per carry to running backs this season. It's better to wait and see whether Jeanty's decrease in carries was punishment for trash-talking the Jets before Week 8 or a sign of what's to come in Klint Kubiak's running back rotation.

Breece Hall, New York Jets (at Kansas City Chiefs)

Hall seems likely to return from a nagging quad injury this week, but he gets a tough matchup against a Chiefs defense that has largely bottled up opposing running backs this season. Hall may also be splitting carries with Braelon Allen, who has thrived in his stead, so this doesn't seem like an ideal spot to trust him.

Aaron Jones and Jordan Mason, Minnesota Vikings (at Buffalo Bills)

We'll do two for the price of one here. The Vikings have a bottom-six rushing offense this season in terms of EPA per play, per the NFL's Next Gen Stats. Jones and Mason are also stuck in a frustrating time share, so neither seems like a particularly viable play against a Bills defense that has allowed just 97.4 rushing yards per game this season.

Michael Wilson, Arizona Cardinals (at Seattle Seahawks)

Wilson finally had a breakout game for the first time since Marvin Harrison Jr. was traded, generating eight catches for 111 yards and a touchdown against the Cowboys. He'll have a much tougher matchup against a Seahawks team that has been excellent against receivers this year, especially after Wilson suffered a couple of broken ribs on his touchdown catch.

Adonai Mitchell, New York Jets (at Kansas City Chiefs)

Mitchell just logged seven catches for 142 yards and a touchdown against the Raiders in Week 8. He gets a much tougher matchup in Week 9 against a Chiefs defense that has allowed the second-fewest FPPG to receivers this season. Add in that Omar Cooper Jr. could also siphon targets from him and Mitchell may be a hit-or-miss play each week even if Garrett Wilson (concussion) is unable to play.

Jalen Coker, Carolina Panthers (vs. Denver Broncos)

The Broncos get a lot of pressure, so Young won't have much time to throw in that matchup. That could limit Coker's deep-ball upside, and while he could rack up yards after the catch, it's hard to trust him to do so against a quality defense.

Khalil Shakir, Buffalo Bills (at Minnesota Vikings)

Shakir started well in the Bills' win over the Ravens but did little outside the first drive. DJ Moore seems likely to continue getting most of the attention from Josh Allen, so don't be surprised if Shakir struggles against a Vikings defense that has allowed the third-fewest FPPG to receivers this season.

Dalton Kincaid, Buffalo Bills (at Minnesota Vikings)

Kincaid scored a touchdown last week for the first time since Week 2, but he will be hard-pressed to repeat that feat against the Vikings. Minnesota has yet to allow a receiving touchdown to tight ends this season, so Kincaid figures to be limited.

T.J. Hockenson, Minnesota Vikings (vs. Buffalo Bills)

The Bills have only allowed one touchdown to tight ends this season. It also isn't clear whether Kyler Murray (hamstring) will play in this contest, so Hockenson might have a lower ceiling in this matchup than most.

No NFL team has allowed fewer points per game than the Vikings (13.3). No NFL team has scored more points per game than the Bills (33.3). Something will give in this matchup, but if there's one quarterback that's built to beat Brian Flores' pressure-happy scheme, it's Josh Allen.

San Francisco 49ers (vs. Las Vegas Raiders)

The 49ers are favored to beat the Raiders, but this could end up being a shootout given how Kirk Cousins and Co. have been playing. Las Vegas is averaging 27.6 points per game, so even if Nick Bosa returns, this is still a low-floor spot for San Francisco.

Klopp explains surprise decision to call up Gladbach prodigy Wael Mohya to Germany squad

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Germany head coach Jürgen Klopp pulled a surprise out of the hat on Thursday by naming 17-year-old Borussia Mönchengladbach prodigy Wael Mohya in his squad for the first time following the youngster's impressive start to the season.

In an interview published by the German Football Association (DFB), Klopp explained his decision to include the highly-rated attacking midfielder.

“He's one of those cases where you simply have to take notice. There aren't many 17-year-olds currently playing in the Bundesliga. That alone is an outstanding achievement. But he's also doing well – really well. I've been in contact with both him and his family, and we've discussed how we want to approach things. I'm really keen to build the future together with these boys, including Wael, without making any promises or anything like that,” he said, adding:

“I would say that I have been working with young, highly talented people for quite some time now, and it is something I am very passionate about. I explained this to the Mohya family, and we then agreed that we would bring him along to give him a chance to experience it and see how things go.”

Klopp went on to stress that there are no guarantees Mohya will make the squad for either of the upcoming games against Serbia and the Netherlands, explaining that the main priority is to see the 17-year-old in training and how he conducts himself as part of the national team setup.

“Above all, the important thing is to let him experience the training sessions firsthand, because I think that is really valuable when you have the opportunity. And we would like to continue doing this in the future whenever possible: always having a place in the squad where we can say, ‘Okay, come along.’ Let's see how he does, whether he plays, and whether he even makes it into any of the matchday squads. Of course, it will always depend on his performances in training. That's only logical,” Klopp said.

"But it's also about seeing how a young guy conducts himself, both on and off the pitch, and having conversations with him. I've had three conversations with him so far, and they've all been excellent, I have to say. I wasn't as self-confident as he is at 17, but then again, I wasn't as good as he is at 17 either! So I'm really looking forward to having Wael with us."

Mohya, who is also eligible to represent Morocco, only made his debut for Germany's U21 side in September.

He has three goals and one assist in 10 games for Gladbach this season.

When will Lionel Messi and Cristiano Ronaldo retire from professional football?

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

When will Lionel Messi and Cristiano Ronaldo bring the curtains down on their careers? The end is nigh for both.

They once ruled the footballing world as the best players of their generation. Their legacies remain strong as two of the best players of all time. The GOAT debates are endless.

But time catches up with everyone and the end is near for Messi’s and Ronaldo’s legendary careers. The question is: how much longer do we have left to enjoy them?

Ronaldo is the older of the pair at 41. Messi recently turned 39. Neither are playing in Europe anymore, of course, with Ronaldo in the Saudi Pro League and Messi in MLS.

Both have now brought their international careers to an end, with Messi having already said goodbye to Argentina and Ronaldo having announced his retirement from Portugal.

Ronaldo’s latest announcement represents a significant milestone in the winding down of his career, although he has yet to confirm whether he’ll play in one last farewell match.

At club level, both players still have time left on their contracts. Ronaldo’s deal with Al-Nassr is due to last until the end of the season. Messi, meanwhile, has a contract until December 2028 with Inter Miami.

Per some reports from when he signed his latest renewal in 2025, Ronaldo’s contract contains an option to extend into the summer of 2028 as well.

Thus, there could be another couple of years left of Messi and Ronaldo as professional footballers.

But contracts aren’t always fulfilled and many factors could influence each player’s future. We just hope injury isn’t the way either go out.

The less outspoken of the two long-time Ballon d’Or rivals, Messi hasn’t given too much away about his future in club football.

“I know that the moment I feel that I am no longer able to perform, that I no longer enjoy or help my team-mates, I will quit,” he offered in March 2024.

“I’m very self-critical. I know when I’m good, when I’m bad, when I’m playing well, and when I’m playing badly.

“When I feel it’s time to take that step, I’ll take it without thinking about age. If I feel good, I’ll always try to keep competing because it’s what I like and what I know how to do.

“I haven’t yet thought of what I’m going to do when I retire. Today, I try to enjoy the day-to-day, the moments, without thinking about life after [football].”

However, the sad news of his father passing away prompted Messi to give a rare update on his future this summer.

“I don’t know how to carry on. I used to just play football, and now I’m really not sure if I’ll carry on doing it for much longer,” he posted on social media.

“You were by my side from the very beginning. We were so close to the end. Why didn’t you hang in there just a little longer so we could finish this together?”

Reflecting on the World Cup after Argentina lost the final to Spain, Messi added: “I wanted to win it so I could bring the trophy to you and show you a new one. I couldn’t, my legs had nothing left. This time I tried to push past my physical limits, but I couldn’t. I never managed to feel right.”

And since, Messi has confirmed he is bowing out from international duty altogether.

He posted on Instagram on August 31: “After the time that has passed since the final, and after giving it a lot of thought, I want to let everyone know that I am retiring from the National Team…

“It was a decision that hurt—and still hurts deep down—but I understand that the time has come. I swear to you that I always gave it my all—not just in these last few years when we won everything, but before that too; I always fought and left everything on the field for this jersey, to bring you joy and make you feel proud to be Argentine through football.

“Time is running short, chapters come to a close, and this is one that hurts me deeply. I love, have loved, and will always love being part of the National Team. I’ve given everything I had; I have nothing left to give, and besides, there are some great young players coming up who deserve to be here.

“Thank you for all the affection over these past 20 years. I will miss hearing you from the pitch so much. Now I’ll be just one of you, cheering and supporting from the outside (through the good times and the bad—especially the bad).

“Thank you to my family for always being there, for accompanying me on this journey—so beautiful, yet so difficult. Thank you to every coach, teammate, and official I’ve shared this path with. I take with me unforgettable memories and moments.

“I leave with the peace of mind and pride of having given you—alongside my teammates—moments we once only dreamed of. It was an incredible experience to live this with you all. I love you!

“Thank you, God, for making me Argentine.

“*I wrote these words on July 21st, two days after the final. Today, after what happened with my dad, I am even more convinced than before.”

And so that’s the end of Messi’s international career, after 207 caps, 125 goals, 68 assists, two Copa Americas and a World Cup.

Ronaldo has been more vocal about the end of his career, partially because of his personality and partially because it’s probably closer.

He hinted in November 2025 that he could retire ‘soon’, before clarifying: “Soon for me means in 10 years… No, I’m joking.

“I’m really enjoying the moment right now. As you know, in football, when you reach some age, you count the months very quick.

“I feel very good in this moment. I score goals, I still feel quick and sharp. I’m enjoying my game in the national team. But of course, let’s be honest. What I mean by soon is probably one or two years.”

He has also confirmed his ongoing pursuit of reaching 1000 career goals will have an impact on when he calls it quits.

“It’s hard to continue playing, but I am motivated,” he said in December.

“My passion is high and I want to continue. It doesn’t matter where I play, whether in the Middle East or Europe. I always enjoy playing football and I want to keep going.

“You know what my goal is. I want to win trophies and I want to reach that number [1000 goals] that you all know. I will reach the number for sure, if no injuries.”

For what it’s worth, Messi could be capable of reaching 1000 goals as well, but it remains to be seen if that will have a bearing on his future.

Ronaldo’s international future, meanwhile, has now been settled. Having previously suggested that he would reflect on his Portugal career after the World Cup, the five-time Ballon d’Or winner has officially announced his retirement from international football.

He made the announcement in a heartfelt farewell message to the Portuguese people, opening with the words: “Portugal will always be a part of me.”

“The time has come to hang up our national team’s number 7 shirt. I never felt it was mine alone, but for 23 years I had the privilege and responsibility of wearing it and defending it in stadiums all around the world,” Ronaldo wrote.

The 41-year-old admitted that the decision was difficult, adding: “Today, the time has come to hand it over, but it was not an easy decision, because some decisions are not made alone.”

Ronaldo’s international career therefore comes to an end after 234 appearances and 146 goals for Portugal, having helped his country win Euro 2016 and two Nations League titles.

That leaves his club future as the biggest remaining question. Ronaldo has made no announcement about retiring from football altogether, and his pursuit of 1000 career goals remains a significant motivation.

His current contract with Al-Nassr expires at the end of the season, by which point he’ll be 42. Every chance he calls it quits then if he scores his thousandth goal before then, which appears likely.

How Kimi Antonelli Trains His Brain for Peak Speed—and an F1 Record

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Sign up below to receive our newest workout routines, recipes, news stories, and offers from our partners

With the right plan and the right discipline, you can get seriously shredded in just 28 days.

At age 62, "Big Bill" shares his wisdom to dominate one of the ultimate strength marks.

Follow these fit women we're crushing on for inspiration, workout ideas, and motivation.

It’s official, 20-year-old Kimi Antonelli has secured his spot as the youngest F1 Champion in history after just two seasons, but while the dedicated Mercedes driver has been relentless on the track, the speedy Italian has also stayed active outside of his car. Here’s why the history maker’s appetite for adventure is the reason that he never stays still, and how a smart approach to staying active benefits both body and mind.

Kimi Antonelli’s third place in Mexico City on November 1 meant that he’d earned enough points to stave off all other drivers to complete the season as champion, but this overnight success is the result of a mature approach to staying sharp. “I try to do things that keep me active, but won’t tire me out,” explained Antonelli during a recent and telling interview with GQ. “It’s all a way to release mental stress, to just relax and enjoy. I try to do these activities as much as possible because in sports, the mental [part] is basically what does everything.”

The activities that Antonelli spoke of include race related training such as simulators, go-karts, and operating remote controlled cars, but he’s also a keen proponent of padel. Not only has the young upstart played against fellow Mercedes drivers like George Russell, but he’s even been known to stroke the ball from the deck of a super yacht from time to time.

Antonelli’s fuel for dominating F1 was on show early, as he transitioned from Formula 2 and continued his approach to “hard” training, sharing that a day of working out included “An early morning swim, neck training at lunch, and an afternoon bike ride.”

The inspirational Italian is also hot with the hoops, and in a recent Instagram Reel revealed that he can make a basket from an impressive distance, all while standing on one leg!

While Antonelli’s love of fun activities helps him keep a positive mindset as he works towards pole position, science has also shown that becoming skilled in a variety of sporting activities can only help with his day job. Activities such as shooting baskets, stroking the Padel ball, and other loves such as surfing, no doubt boost his reaction times. In fact, a scientific study in 2023 confirmed that reaction training (they used flashing lights as the stimuli) induced beneficial effects on physiological and cognitive performance.

At just 20 years of age, Kimi Antonelli has the world at his highly successful feet. So, what’s next? “Right now, my main goal is to give everything to F1, but in my normal life, I want to be successful, too,” he revealed to GQ. “I want to be efficient, and in 20 to 30 years’ time, when I won’t be racing anymore, I want to still do something that keeps me active. Because once you finish, it’s easy to do nothing after working hard for so many years. I want to be successful outside of racing.”

With an attitude like Antonelli’s, the journey is far from over.

Cristiano Ronaldo makes bombshell retirement decision after stunning Portugal suspension

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Cristiano Ronaldo has retired from international duty with Portugal.

The country’s greatest ever player announced his decision via a video to camera on his social media channels.

Ronaldo scored 146 goals in 234 games for Portugal, becoming the all-time leading scorer in the history of men’s international football.

Between 2003 and 2026, a storied international career delivered three trophies: the European Championship title in France in 2016 and two UEFA Nations League titles, in 2019 and 2025.

Ronaldo said in his social media address that the “time had come” to hand back the No7 shirt but that he had “never felt it belonged to me alone”.

The 41-year-old considered retiring after this summer’s World Cup but was persuaded to stay on by Portuguese FA president Pedro Proenca, a former UEFA referee.

But Ronaldo left Portugal’s training camp on September 30 after a fallout with head coach Jorge Jesus when he was asked to warm up midway through a Nations League game against Norway but was never brought on as a substitute.

Rediscover your adventurous side on an exotic getaway

In a long October 6 statement that ran to 2,000 words, the Al Nassr striker accused Portugal’s head coach of breaking promises but also apologised and expressed a keeness to return to the international fold.

On October 10, the Portuguese FA provisionally suspended Ronaldo pending disciplinary proceedings, which followed on October 19. He was then fined £215 and suspended for 30 days, during which time Portugal had no matches scheduled.

He was expected to return to the national team for this month’s Nations League matches against Denmark and Wales but has now announced his international retirement.

It means his final international break with Portugal (the window in September and October) was the same as his great rival Lionel Messi’s final international break with Argentina. Both players are to now play club football only.

Ronaldo made his international debut in August 2003 at the age of 18 and bows out from Portugal duty at the age of 41 having eclipsed Eusebio to become the greatest player in his country’s history.

Ashlee Simpson Will Be Drinking Liquid I.V. Hot Chocolate and Listening to Mariah Carey This Christmas

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The "Pieces of Me" singer collaborated with the hydration brand for limited-edition holiday flavors and a festive mug she helped design.

All products and services featured are independently chosen by editors. However, Billboard may receive a commission on orders placed through its retail links, and the retailer may receive certain auditable data for accounting purposes.

“The holiday season is my favorite time,” Ashlee Simpson tells Billboard, but it’s also one of her busiest. Between being a mom of three, Christmas shopping and working on new music, there is plenty keeping her on her toes. That’s why she starts decorating in November. “It all goes so fast, so if I do December, and we travel during the holidays, then I don’t get to enjoy it.” Related Cher Hits Michael Douglas With Kindness After Actor's 'Moonstruck' Oscar Diss Hailey Bieber Details Emotional Rollercoaster of PMDD Diagnosis After Son Jack Blues' Birth: 'Just a Really Intense and Special Journey' Barbie's Diwali Doll Is Back in Stock for the Festival of Lights: Here's Where to Find It Online Explore Ashlee Simpson See latest videos, charts and news

This year, Simpson is adding a little extra comfort to the chaos with her Liquid I.V. collaboration, starring in the brand’s holiday campaign for its limited-edition Cozy Caramel Hot Chocolate. While many know Liquid I.V. for its hydration benefits (particularly the morning after one too many Baileys, IYKYK), the brand is giving its signature formula a festive twist.

While Simpson says her kids love the holiday flavor too, sitting down with a warm cup is an excuse to make a little space for herself. “That’s the time when I put on my Christmas music and have a little moment,” she says. As to her favorite Christmas music? “Obviously, Mariah. But I start Christmas music in October,” she laughs. “I’m that person. I love all the cheesy, happy songs — the Home Alone songs — I love all of it.”

Ashlee Simpson likes to warm up her Cozy Caramel Hot Chocolate Liquid I.V. and add a candy cane for an extra festive touch.

Liquid I.V.’s holiday series features two limited-edition flavors: Cozy Caramel and Hot Chocolate. Made with real cocoa, no artificial sweeteners or colors and 440mg of electrolytes, the blends offer a seasonal take on the brand’s Hydration Multiplier formula. Shoppers can also buy the three-pack bundle for $26.94, which includes six sticks per pack or 18 sticks in total.

Beyond the drinks, Simpson also helped design an exclusive holiday mug with the brand. “The mugs are definitely a stocking stuffer this year,” she says.

The limited rollout of collectible holiday mugs comes bundled with two cartons of Hydration Multiplier Cozy Caramel Hot Chocolate and two cartons of Hydration Multiplier Hot Chocolate.

Featuring a black design with a blue snowflake, the collectible has a grunge-meets-festive aesthetic that feels a little different from your typical Christmas mug, and we love it.

With just 1,000 available, a portion of proceeds from the mug bundle, up to $10,000, will support the brand’s Impact Program, which helps provide clean water solutions through partnerships with nonprofit organizations nationwide.

This time of year also marks a milestone for Simpson, who celebrated 21 years of her sophomore album, I Am Me, which came out in October 2005. “That album was definitely a defining moment in my life and in my career,” she tells Billboard. “I feel like I was in a moment of really standing up for myself and finding myself on a deeper level and also a vulnerable level, with songs like ‘Catch Me When I Fall.’ There was a vulnerability with what I had gone through in my career.”

When asked what she would tell her 2005 self today, Simpson says, “I would just give her a hug. ‘Don’t be hard on yourself, you’ve got this.’ And I feel like during that time I was really in that place of just fighting for myself, and I would be very proud of that version of myself.” As for what’s next musically, Simpson has been busy writing and recording, telling us, “I’ve been in the studio, I’ve been writing a bunch. I’m trying to finish up a bunch of stuff before the holidays are here.”

Liquid I.V. uses a science-backed formula featuring an optimized ratio of electrolytes, essential vitamins and clinically tested nutrients to support hydration. Its primary electrolytes, sodium and potassium, help the body absorb water. According to the brand, Hydration Multiplier is designed to hydrate faster than water alone and contains three times the electrolytes of the leading sports drink. The formula is caffeine-free but includes B vitamins, which can promote energy.

Liam Fitzgerald, a popular online coach and fitness influencer has passed away at just 29 years of age after injuries sustained during a fatal motorcycle accident. Tributes have poured in for the star, and his family have hailed him as an inspirational figure.

Fitzgerald, who was born in Evansville, Wisconsin, and was a social media influencer and OnlyFans creator, died on October 30, per announcement by his girlfriend Jamie Lyn Rinaldi. “It is with the heaviest heart that I share the devastating news that Liam passed away following a motorcycle accident on October 30th at approximately 2 AM,” she wrote via Instagram. “He suffered severe injuries, and despite the efforts of those who tried desperately to save him, they were unable to bring him back. There are no words to describe the heartbreak of this loss.”

Fitzgerald was an athlete before becoming an influencer and after a move to Washington state, his teenage years were highly active. Fitzgerald played football as a quarterback for the Camas Papermakers, skateboarded, and developed a love of working out. He later relocated to Arizona and finally settled in Miami, Florida, where he built an Instagram base of more than one million followers by sharing his fitness, fashion, and lifestyle advice.

Fitzgerald also collaborated with well known brands such as YoungLA, who posted a tribute to the star via its official Instagram account. “He brought an incredible energy, joy, and excitement to every shoot, making every moment that much more special,” read the statement. “His presence will be deeply missed, and we are grateful to have had the opportunity to work alongside him.”

The influencer’s family has also spoken about Fitzgerald’s struggles with alcohol. “This is shared in the hope that his story reaches someone who needs it,” read a statement. “He wasn’t perfect and never pretended to be. But he always strived to grow and do better. He had dreams, ambitions, and so much more life to live. He had just turned 29. So many places to see, memories to make, and people to love. His life was tragically cut short in a motorcycle accident, leaving behind a heartbreak that words will never be able to describe.”

Rather the Gunners are now said to have their eyes on Alex Baena, in addition to the likes of Kenan Yildiz and Antonio Nusa. Follow the latest transfer news, rumours and gossip live below!

Cristiano Ronaldo has retired from international duty with Portugal.

The country’s greatest-ever player announced his decision via a video to camera on his social media channels.

Ronaldo netted 146 goals in 234 games for Portugal, becoming the all-time leading scorer in the history of men’s international football.

The striker was suspended by the Portuguese FA last month after he stormed out of the Nations League camp in October, having fallen out with manager Jorge Jesus.

Chelsea are interested in signing Everton star Harrison Armstrong, reports claim.

The England Under-21 international has established a reputation as one of the Premier League’s most impressive young midfielders under David Moyes.

TEAMtalk cite reports claiming that Chelsea are compiling weekly scouting reports on the 19-year-old, who continues to go from strength to strength at the Hill Dickinson Stadium.

Chelsea are widely expected to swoop for another top young midfielder in January in Bournemouth’s Alex Scott, 23.

Harry Kane’s bumper new Bayern Munich contract has now been confirmed.

The Ballon d’Or winner has been in talks with the reigning Bundesliga champions for months now regarding a lucrative extension to his previous deal that was due to expire next summer.

Kane is now tied to Bayern until 2029 on a record-breaking contract that is said to make him the highest-paid player in Europe by salary.

The England captain will be 36 by the time it expires, meaning he may well now never return to the Premier League and looks destined to stay second on the list of all-time top scorers behind Alan Shearer.

Manchester United looks set to make a sensational loan swoop for Ivan Toney in January, reports claim.

The struggling Red Devils are desperate for more firepower under Michael Carrick with first-choice striker Benjamin Sesko out until January with his lingering shin injury.

According to Football Insider, United want England star Toney, who now plays his football in Saudi Arabia with Al-Ahli.

They are more likely to make a loan move for the former Brentford frontman than a permanent offer, however.

Arsenal could be lining up a shock move for an Atletico Madrid star that ISN’T Julian Alvarez.

The Gunners chased a high-profile deal for the Argentine striker throughout the closing stages of a hectic summer transfer window and are understood to have retained their interest in his services.

However, it seems that one of Alvarez’s team-mates at the Metropolitano Stadium has now caught Mikel Arteta’s eye.

According to TEAMtalk, Arsenal are keeping close tabs on highly-rated Spain midfielder Alex Baena, though could face serious competition for his signing.

Gunter joined the runaway National League leaders over the summer and immediately secured his spot as their first-choice option, but has returned to Spurs to train with the first team following an injury to third-choice goalkeeper Brandon Austin.

Standard Sport understands that Gunter will continue to be around first-team training before looking to go back out on loan when the January transfer window opens.

Gunter is one of several academy talents at Tottenham who are assessing their options ahead of the January window, with Tyrese Hall expected to have both loan and permanent offers on the table.

Under-21s captain James Rowswell is another player who will be potentially looking to leave Spurs on loan in the New Year, having failed to secure a summer switch.

Celta Vigo defender Javi Rodriguez is attracting interest from a number of clubs across Europe, writes Sam Tabuteau.

Despite reports, though, there has not yet been any contact from Fulham for the 23-year-old.

Rodriguez made his Spain debut in June and is expected to attract interest in January.

Real Madrid are confident of winning the race to sign Manchester United teenager JJ Gabriel, who has not returned to Old Trafford since leaving the club in September.

The 16-year-old is currently training independently in Spain after deciding to pursue a pathway towards senior football elsewhere.

Barcelona and Paris Saint-Germain have also shown interest, but Jose Mourinho’s Real Madrid are considered the leading contenders, according to BBC Sport.

Gabriel has impressed at youth level, scoring a hat-trick on his UEFA Youth League debut, and has obtained an EU passport that could ease a move abroad.

United would receive compensation if he leaves, although the fee would be lower for an overseas transfer.

Carrick will now hope his team have momentum ahead of a crucial clash with Aston Villa on Sunday before the international break. The head coach will now that he is not yet out of the woods.

That stance is not expected to change despite a much-needed victory.

Man Utd chiefs are said to be happy for Carrick to remain in charge until the international break, at which point a new manager could be appointment and given extra time to settle in the job before domestic football return.

Comparing Lionel Messi and Cristiano Ronaldo’s final international stats

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

A major chapter in the Lionel Messi vs Cristiano Ronaldo rivalry is drawing to a close, with Messi having bade Argentina farewell and Ronaldo having now announced his retirement from international football with Portugal.

With both players’ international careers now over, their total tallies for Portugal and Argentina are finalised. How do they compare on the international stage?

After a legendary career with Argentina, in which he’s comfortably their all-time top goalscorer and appearance-maker, the 39-year-old captain waved goodbye to the fans with a goal and two assists against Benin.

Ronaldo has now confirmed that his time with Portugal is also over, bringing the curtain down on an extraordinary international career. Whether he’ll be given the opportunity to enjoy a farewell appearance in front of his home supporters remains to be seen.

The 41-year-old leaves international football as the most prolific goalscorer in men’s international history, having represented Portugal at five World Cups and six European Championships.

Few players will ever get close to the international numbers Messi and Ronaldo have left behind. Ronaldo finishes with 234 Portugal appearances and 146 goals, both higher than Messi’s final totals of 207 caps and 125 goals for Argentina.

Ronaldo was also the more prolific goalscorer – both in terms of his overall tally and averages. His 146 strikes arrived at one every 127 minutes, compared with Messi’s rate of one every 136. Taking penalties out of the equation leaves Ronaldo ahead again, averaging a goal every 151 minutes to Messi’s 172.

Messi’s advantage comes in the creative department. He provided 67 assists for Argentina, comfortably clear of Ronaldo’s 37, and that swings the overall goal contribution numbers his way: 193 to 183.

There is very little separating them elsewhere. Messi scored 12 direct free-kicks to Ronaldo’s 11 and registered 11 hat-tricks to Ronaldo’s 10. He was also, surprisingly, more reliable from the spot, converting 78% of his penalties compared with Ronaldo’s 69%.

Add everything together, and Messi averaged a goal or assist every 89 minutes of his international career. Ronaldo’s corresponding figure is every 101 minutes, despite his superior individual scoring rate.

Messi also finishes one ahead in the trophy count. Argentina won four major honours during his career, including the World Cup and two Copa Americas, while Ronaldo helped Portugal claim Euro 2016 and two Nations League titles.

Here’s the full breakdown of Messi and Ronaldo’s stats on the international stage:

Appearances: 208 Goals: 126 Assists: 67 ⭐ Goal Contributions: 193 ⭐

Penalties scored/taken: 26/33 ⭐ (78% ⭐) Free-kicks: 12 ⭐ Hat-tricks: 11 ⭐

Minutes per goal: 136.5 Minutes per non-penalty goal: 172.1 Minutes per goal or assist: 89.1 ⭐

1 x FIFA World Cup 1 x Finalissima 2 x Copa America

Appearances: 234 ⭐ Goals: 146 ⭐ Assists: 37 Goal Contributions: 183

Penalties scored/taken: 23/33 (69% ⭐) Free-kicks: 11 Hat-tricks: 10

Minutes per goal: 127 ⭐ Minutes per non-penalty goal: 151 ⭐ Minutes per goal or assist: 101

1 x UEFA European Championship 2 x UEFA Nations League

Hojlund: ‘I disagree’ with Allegri over Napoli performance, everyone must ‘take responsibility’

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Rasmus Hojlund ‘disagrees’ with his coach, Massimiliano Allegri, over Napoli’s performance against Porto and adds: ‘We must look at ourselves in the mirror; not only players, everybody in the football club needs to take responsibility.

Napoli suffered a 4-1 away loss to Porto in the Champions League on Wednesday and have earned only three points in the opening four games of the competition, winning once and losing three times.

The Partenopei have already lost seven games from 14 across all competitions.

“It’s difficult to speak about it. I think we are in a very dark place right now, and we need to work. We are together and see if we can improve,” Hojlund told Amazon Prime Italia at full time.

Allegri tried not to make a drama out of the defeat, saying that the performance was good, at least for the opening 70 minutes.

“I disagree; I think, of course, it’s not a bad game. I can agree with this, but we have to stop conceding bad goals, and we have to start winning football matches. We’ve lost too many games this season,” Hojlund replied.

The Denmark international looked disheartened during his post-match interview, and when he was asked how he felt, his reply was honest: “Disappointed, angry, of course, like you said.

“Perhaps because I’ve been a bit sick, I’m a bit tired now after 90 minutes, but we must look at ourselves in the mirror; not only players, everybody in the football club needs to take responsibility.”

The atmosphere in the Napoli dressing room appears to be turning toxic, as Matteo Politano’s post-match interview with Sky Sport Italia and the ugly confrontation between Lorenzo Lucca and Kevin De Bruyne during the match show, when the Italian striker literally told his teammate to “f*** off.”

Lorenzo Bettoni is the Editor of Football Italia.

Cristiano Ronaldo QUITS international football over explosive fall-out with Portugal manager: Superstar, 41, announces retirement in emotional five-minute video

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 05:34 EDT, 5 November 2026 | Updated: 06:26 EDT, 5 November 2026

Cristiano Ronaldo has called time on his illustrious career in international football.

Ronaldo, 41, scored a world record 146 goals in 234 games for Portugal and won the 2016 European Championship as well as two Nations League titles.

His retirement comes just weeks after his explosive falling-out with new manager Jorge Jesus, which ended with Ronaldo abandoning Portugal's camp during the most recent international break in September and October.

It appeared as if he could return for the upcoming break this month for clashes with Denmark and Wales, but he has decided to step away after 23 years.

In his retirement statement, he admitted that he had at times 'failed' and revealed that his decision was difficult, but felt proud of his work and willing to hand over the number seven jersey.

The Al-Nassr star is hoping to pass the baton to his son, 16-year-old Cristiano Junior, who this week has been called up for Portugal's Under-17s.

Ronaldo made his debut for Portugal in 2003 aged 18 and went on to play in six World Cups, a record he shares with Lionel Messi, as well as six European Championships, two Nations League finals, and a Confederations Cup.

Cristiano Ronaldo has called time on his illustrious international career with Portugal

The highlight of his Portugal career was lifting the 2016 European Championship, even if he got injured in the final

He never reached a World Cup final and cried after Portugal were knocked out by Spain in the last 16 over the summer

Ironically, his most memorable hour in a Portugal shirt was arguably when he wasn't playing. After getting injured during the 2016 Euros final, he cut such a passionate figure on the touchline, barking instructions to his team-mates, that he had practically taken over from manager Fernando Santos.

However, his dream of winning a World Cup always eluded him, and his best results were reaching the semi-finals in 2006 in Germany and the quarters in 2022 in Qatar.

His final act in football's greatest tournament was Portugal's 1-0 defeat by arch-rivals Spain at this summer's World Cup – a devastating day which saw him leave in tears.

And his final minutes in a Portugal shirt came against Wales in September, when he played 67 minutes in a 1-0 Nations League win over Wales.

After being left on the bench for their subsequent match against Norway, Ronaldo had a row with manager Jesus and flew away from Portugal's camp via his private jet.

Marking his retirement, he wrote: 'To the people of Portugal, the day has come to return the number 7 jersey of our national team. I never felt it was exclusively mine. But for 23 years, I had the privilege and the responsibility to wear it, to defend it, and to take it with me to stadiums all over the world.

'Today has come the moment to hand it over to the future. It was not an easy decision, because some decisions are not made just with the head, they are also made with the heart.

'I was always demanding of myself. I always wanted more, I always believed we could go further. And I gave everything I had for this jersey. There were perfect afternoons and nights, there were victories I will never forget.

The five-time Ballon d'Or winner admitted he had at times 'failed' but was still proud

His time with Portugal in the last international break ended acrimoniously as he abandoned their camp after being left on the bench against Norway

Manager Jorge Jesus insisted that Ronaldo is welcome back, and did his team-mates

'There were defeats that even today I find hard to remember. I also failed, I also suffered, there were also moments when I was not able to give the Portuguese people what they so desired. But I never gave up. And above all, I never stopped feeling proud to represent Portugal.

'Throughout these years, I learned very simple things. Goals pass, trophies stay in museums, records end up being broken. But there is something that time can never erase: the place we occupy in people's hearts.

'If I ever made you happy, if ever a goal of mine made you hug someone, forget a problem, or simply feel proud of our country, then it was all worth it. Because being Portuguese is also that. We suffer together, we get up together.

'If I got this far, it was because we were always hand in hand. And to the Portuguese, to all Portuguese people scattered around the world, I want to tell you one last thing. There is an image I will always carry with me: the moment the national anthem begins. I look around, I see my teammates, I see the stands, I see children, mothers, fathers, and grandfathers, I see Portuguese people who came from so far just to be there.

'And during those minutes, we stop being different. There are no clubs, there are no colors, there are no classes, there are no professions, there are no opinions. There is only one passion: Portugal.'

There will be questions over the way Ronaldo's international career has ended – to the public, it appears to be on rather bitter terms.

Ronaldo was livid after being left on the bench despite warming-up for Portugal's Nations League win over Norway on September 27 – the first time he has been an unused substitute during his 18-year international career.

That led to him skipping three training sessions and abandoning the camp for Portugal's last two matches of the international break, fleeing to Spain via private jet.

The 41-year-old has scored scored 146 goals in 234 games - the most of anybody ever

He did not even watch their next match against Norway because he was soaring away on his plane with his family.

The former Manchester United star was vexed at how Jesus had handled his press conference after leaving him on the bench.

Ronaldo insisted that Jesus had broken a promise to apologise to the 'Portuguese people' and his team-mates for not bringing him on.

He claimed he had been guaranteed 30 minutes of football against Norway after the Portuguese FA persuaded him not to retire following the World Cup.

The five-time Ballon d'Or winner even offered to retire if that was what the coach and head of Portuguese FA wanted, only for them to reiterate their desire to have him.

But most hurtfully, Ronaldo released a stinging 1,996-word statement accusing Jesus of 'broken promises'. He said the manager had agreed to deliver an eight-point mea culpa taking responsibility for leaving him on the bench after a supposed pact to play him.

Jesus did concede it was a mistake to make him warm up but gave a different narrative of events, saying: 'The match in Norway was not part of the plan I drew up for him. However, I felt that he had managed to recover physically from the first match and I warned him that I might have to use him. Cristiano agreed.'

In the end, Ronaldo was fined £215 by the Portuguese FA and technically banned for a month – though that was backdated, making him free for their upcoming fixtures.

It was a messy episode – not unlike how he left Manchester United for a second time in 2022 – and the question remains as to whether he would have still been playing had the row never occurred.

'To the people of Portugal, the day has come to return the number 7 jersey of our national team. I never felt it was exclusively mine. But for 23 years, I had the privilege and the responsibility to wear it, to defend it, and to take it with me to stadiums all over the world.

'Today has come the moment to hand it over to the future. It was not an easy decision, because some decisions are not made just with the head, they are also made with the heart.

'A journey comes to an end that began in August 2003 and changed my life forever. When I look back and think about everything I experienced in the national team, I do not just see games, goals, records, or trophies. I see people. I see immigrants who, for 90 minutes, felt Portugal a little closer.

'I see millions of Portuguese people that I never met personally, but who were part of my life. Because at the end of everything, football is much greater than a game. Football is people. Throughout my career in the national team, I sought to surpass my limits. I wanted to win, I wanted to be the best, I wanted to make history.

'I was always demanding of myself. I always wanted more, I always believed we could go further. And I gave everything I had for this jersey. There were perfect afternoons and nights, there were victories I will never forget.

'There were defeats that even today I find hard to remember. I also failed, I also suffered, there were also moments when I was not able to give the Portuguese people what they so desired. But I never gave up. And above all, I never stopped feeling proud to represent Portugal.

'Throughout these years, I learned very simple things. Goals pass, trophies stay in museums, records end up being broken. But there is something that time can never erase: the place we occupy in people's hearts.

'If I ever made you happy, if ever a goal of mine made you hug someone, forget a problem, or simply feel proud of our country, then it was all worth it. Because being Portuguese is also that. We suffer together, we get up together.

'We believe again. Portugal will always need youngsters who dream of it, players who never give up, new generations who believe it is possible to reach where no one has reached. Because a country that continues to create new heroes is a country that never stops believing in itself.

'To my national team teammates, I want to leave a special word. Thank you for every run, for every hug, for every argument, for every silence before entering the pitch. For every victory we celebrated together and for every defeat that made us stronger. I had the privilege of being your captain, but no captain walks alone. And you are part of my history. To all the coaches, directors, doctors, physiotherapists, kit men, staff, and so many people who worked with me over these years, my thank you.

'Many of you never appeared in these photographs. But without you, none of those photographs would have been possible. To my family, thank you for sharing this dream with me. Thank you for understanding the absences, the sacrifices, and the moments when the national team had to occupy a space that also belonged to you.

'If I got this far, it was because we were always hand in hand. And to the Portuguese, to all Portuguese people scattered around the world, I want to tell you one last thing. There is an image I will always carry with me: the moment the national anthem begins. I look around, I see my teammates, I see the stands, I see children, mothers, fathers, and grandfathers, I see Portuguese people who came from so far just to be there.

'And during those minutes, we stop being different. There are no clubs, there are no colors, there are no classes, there are no professions, there are no opinions. There is only one passion: Portugal.

'That is a feeling I will carry with me for the rest of my life. Today, my journey on the pitch with the national team ends. I leave with my head held high, proud of the path I walked and with the awareness that I gave everything I had for Portugal.

'But I will never stop being one of you. I will continue to suffer, to cheer, and to celebrate every victory of Portugal. I spent my life working to be the best player in the world. Today I realize that the greatest victory was having deserved to represent the best country in the world.

'Thank you for these 23 years. Thank you for believing in me. Thank you for allowing me to live a dream that began when I was just a boy in Madeira, chasing a ball. The number seven jersey stays, the pride of having worn it will be mine forever. Portugal will always be in me. Farewell always, Cristiano Ronaldo.'

Fabregas: ‘A wonderful night for Como, but must stay humble’ after 9-1 victory

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Cesc Fabregas admits the 9-1 win in Lens was ‘a wonderful night for Como, but we need to keep our feet on the ground’ as Champions League ambition starts to genuinely build.

The Lariani had won both their home games 4-1 against RB Leipzig and Manchester United, but lost 3-2 to Feyenoord, so everyone was waiting for their first ever Champions League away victory.

What they did not expect was for this to end 9-1 in Lens, with a Tasos Douvikas hat-trick, Nico Paz, Lucas Da Cunha, Marc-Oliver Kempf, Mattia Liberali, Martin Baturina and teenage debutant Matteo Papaccioli on target.

It was noted that Como are fourth in the big Champions League table midway through the league phase of the competition.

“It is also our fourth ever Champions League game, so four is the key number here,” smiled Fabregas on Sky Sport Italia.

“I’m happy, this is football and everything went our way. The lads did well, now we have to prepare for a very important match on Sunday, the ninth in a run between breaks for international duty.”

This was the sixth consecutive victory in all competition for Como, who will face Scudetto holders Inter in Serie A this weekend.

It was a truly historic occasion, as no Italian team had ever scored even seven away goals in the Champions League or its predecessor the European Cup, let alone nine.

“It is a wonderful night for Como, for all our fans and many lads here who are at the start of their careers, but they need to keep their feet on the ground, be humble, keep learning and not think everything will go well from here on.

“They must keep working to continue these performances.”

Douvikas has also now scored in each of his four Champions League games and is the current top scorer in the tournament.

However, Lens scored with a volley on a corner kick, so are yet to keep a single clean sheet in the Champions League.

“I am angry over the goal we conceded, as we changed something in our defending with the three substitutions,” confessed Fabregas.

“Plus it was scored by a former teammate of mine, I played with Ruben Aguilar at Monaco, and I told him I’d never seen you score a goal like that before! It was honestly a great goal.”

These weren’t just nine goals, but almost all of them were well-worked team affairs playing out from the back with a string of passes, the kind of football that Fabregas wants to see.

Now they will play AEK Athens at home, Real Betis away, concluding with Paris Saint-Germain and then visiting Barcelona on January 27.

“When I saw the last two games were PSG and Barcelona, I thought we needed to get as many points as possible in the early fixtures. Nothing is impossible, but these are two teams who are of a superior level to the rest.

“There is a long way to go, those are the last two weeks in January, so all I am focused on is recovering the injured players, because we are missing so many in attack especially.

“There’s a final sprint on Sunday before we will try to rest as much as possible over the break for international duty,” noted Fabregas.

It was noted there are eight left-footed players, with five of them on the field together this evening, and this is something unusual when constructing a team, with only Arsenal perhaps packing the squad with that many.

“I have a weakness for left-footed players. I think they tend to be special, those I played with tended to have a class and technique different to the others, so when playing with or against those, that interested me.

“Having said that, we didn’t plan to get so many left-footed players, it just happened. The most important thing is that they understand the football we want to play here, and today everything went our way.

“I want the whole city of Como to enjoy this moment, but from tomorrow we begin preparing for Sunday.”

Pat McAfee is a problem for ESPN's "College GameDay," Joe Scarborough of "Morning Joe" hammered away at Tuesday during election day.

"I've just had enough of this Pat McAfee ruining 'College GameDay,'" Scarborough said Tuesday. "I look forward to waking up on Saturday mornings and watching that. ... It's like he's melting down. ...

"It's really ESPN. I mean, they run their company the way they want to run their company, but man, it is a shame for the viewer."

McAfee revealed there have been death threats against him as a result of the controversy surrounding ESPN's "College GameDay" visit to Utah.

"I've stayed away from my mentions and DMs this month since these back to back days but ... I'm sure if I went in there now, I'd find some wishes for and threats of death for me," McAfee posted on X. "'Don't read them.' They're everywhere."

The post includes screenshots from direct messages threatening violence against him and tweets expressing a desire to see him dead.

The issue, according to Scarborough, is the show has gotten away from college football being played on Saturday and it's more about itself.

"I love watching Rece (Davis)," he said. "I love watching those guys and watching Nick (Saban) on there talking. ... Kirk Herbstreit's amazing. Desmond (Howard) — they're all great. And they're all being eclipsed by ESPN's just insatiable desire to give this guy more time."

I Tried Hailey Bieber’s Favorite Creatine Gummies for Two Months, and I Have Thoughts

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Why these candylike gumdrops have converted me into a creatine-lover—plus, the other formulas I love

The supplement landscape has never been murkier, more confusing, or more overwhelming. While I have a pretty extensive wellness regimen, it feels like there are a million and one powders, pills, and gummies I “should” be adding to my routine. One supplement that keeps coming into the spotlight is creatine.

I first heard about creatine from my 62-year-old mother, who waxed poetic about the supplement. As someone who isn’t one to experiment with supplements, my ears perked up. Then, the influencer of all influencers, Hailey Bieber, posted on Instagram a few weeks later touting the same supplement—this time, a gummy version from Arrae. I thought: That’s it, I’m taking the creatine plunge.

“Creatine works like an energy recycler,” says Roxanne Pero, a Dallas-based obstetrician and gynecologist and part of O Positiv’s Medical Advisory Board. “Every cell in the body needs energy, and that energy comes from a molecule called ATP, or adenosine triphosphate. Creatine helps the body regenerate ATP more quickly, especially when energy runs low, and especially in tissues that use a lot of it, like muscle and the brain.”

As someone who works out five times a week, often in heated classes that push my body to depletion, I figured I was the perfect candidate for creatine. However, creatine is one of those supplements anyone can benefit from; it kicks in when our cells need more energy, like during strenuous exercise, but also during sleep deprivation and stress—and even perimenopause, menopause, and postpartum for women—which everyone experiences at some point.

“Active women in their 20s and 30s may benefit from improved training performance, while women approaching menopause or experiencing it may be interested in supporting muscle strength as part of a broader strategy that includes resistance training and adequate protein,” says Rachel Brief, a registered dietitian and wellness advisor at the Lanby. However, the supplement has gained traction among women in their 40s and above. “As a woman enters menopause and postmenopause, the decrease in estrogen contributes to age-related muscle and strength loss,” says Shilpa Raut, the vice president of R&D at Cymbiotika, who holds a master’s degree and a PhD in pharmaceutical sciences. Creatine can help women maintain muscle mass, support steady metabolic health, and improve cognitive resilience, which often declines with age.

If you’re wondering why everyone isn’t already taking this “magic” supplement, well, I was too. I quickly discovered that this “gym bro” supplement had a bad reputation for causing bloating, which is not a risk I’m willing to take as a girl with a sensitive gut. However, Bieber’s favorite creatine, Arrae’s Tone gummies, are known for the benefits without the bloat. Not to mention, the brand swaps the typical chalky, white powder that creatine conjures for candylike gummies. “Creatine had always been marketed to men, because no one had ever presented it in a way that felt like it was made for women,” Arrae’s cofounder Siffat Haider tells me. “Our customers adopted it immediately, and that demand is why we kept building on the flavors.”

The one caveat with creatine is that you need to drink water to get the full benefits. “Creatine draws water into muscle cells, and that is part of how it works,” says Michael Fredericson, a sports-medicine physiatrist, professor at Stanford University, and Elysium Scientific Advisory Board member. If you’re dehydrated, creatine will pull your body’s existing water supply into your muscles, causing dehydration and hindering normal bodily functions.

After I incorporated Arrae Tone into my routine for two months and subsequently told people I started taking creatine, everyone asked me the same question: “Does it actually work?” My verdict: I don’t plan to stop taking creatine. The Arrae gummies have no chalky taste and are easy to swallow. I take a lot of different powders and pills, so having a delicious gummy reminiscent of my childhood is a nice change of pace. Plus, the dosage is only 3.75 to 5 grams (depending on whether you take three or four gummies), which is on the lower end of the dosage spectrum and has been proven to be effective without causing bloating. Plus, it’s creatine monohydrate, which is the gold standard of creatine, Brief explains.

Experts say creatine is especially beneficial as a supplement to strength-training workouts, so I was excited to see how it amplified my workouts. Even a few weeks into taking creatine, I noticed more definition in my abdominals and arms, areas where I’ve historically struggled to see muscle. When I want to add a powder to a smoothie or my water to boost hydration, I love the Elysium Health powders; they’re only 5 grams, so they’re easy on my stomach, and they come in delicious fruity flavors that taste like nostalgic lemonade.

Kylie Kelce Reveals the One Thing That Makes Someone the Perfect Houseguest

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Kylie Kelce has revealed what a visitor needs to do to be the perfect guest in their home—and it has to do with impressing her and her NFL-star husband Jason Kelce's daughters.

On a recent episode of her podcast, "Not Gonna Lie," the mom of four revealed that their daughters— Finnley, 18 months, Bennett, 3, Elliotte, 5, and Wyatt, 7—love having guests read to them.

“I know one way to my girls’ hearts, and really kids, I always tell people you don’t need to bring gifts when you come to our house. If you come in, you sit down, you offer to read our girls a book, they will be like forever," she revealed.

Kelce added, “Like genuinely, we have obviously family [that] will come and they’ll bring this and they’ll bring that, but if when you come and you sit down, you read a book, I think it’s one of the best ways to connect with kids.”

The glimpse of her houseguest request came just days after she defended her and her spouse's decision to snap up multiple Philadelphia properties right next to each other.

In an episode of her podcast, which featured guest Nikki Glaser, Kelce, 34, and the comedian chatted about hate comments.

"Do you ever get in the comments? Do you ever get feisty?” Glaser asked.

In her reply, Kelce detailed an Instagram account that frequently posts information about their real estate.

“One of the ones that I did it—which was not to a person so it doesn’t really feel as aggressive—was like ‘Celeb Homes.’ They put a bird’s-eye view of where we live with our children, and they were trying to give details of our property," she said.

The account then revealed that the Kelce family had also purchased the home "next door."

“[They were like], ‘Why would you need this many houses?’ And I’m like, ‘for f---ing security and privacy, you pieces of s--t.' They left the post up, so it’s still there. You can still find it," Kelce explained.

“‘Why would anyone need three neighboring properties?’ For safety, privacy, and security. Get the f outta here," reads the comment Kelce left.

She then told the comedian, "I think I typed ‘get f---ed.’ And then when I tried to post it, it wouldn’t let me. Why? I want to tell people to get f---ed."

Her admission came after she offered a glimpse of the family's childcare arrangements during an appearance on an episode of "IMO with Michelle Obama and Craig Robinson." She explained that she and the former Philadelphia Eagles star prefer to keep their relatives closely involved in raising their children.

The couple have built a support system that allows them to balance their professional commitments with family life.

A major part of that arrangement involves Kelce's parents, Lisa and Ed McDevitt, who live just 15 minutes from the family's Philadelphia-area home.

Kelce explained that her parents regularly help care for the girls, making it possible for her and Jason to navigate their busy schedules without relying on round-the-clock childcare.

Although the couple do employ a nanny, Kelce revealed that the arrangement is limited to traditional working hours rather than having someone live in their home.

“My husband and I have very busy schedules. We don’t necessarily want a live-in nanny or someone who’s going to be raising our kids void of us. That feels weird. So we do a lot with my parents every chance we get. We do things with Jason’s parents. We find that those are the values that we were raised with that we want our kids to have, not just because we tell them that, but because we live it," she explained.

The decision, she explained, reflects their desire to remain actively involved in their daughters' everyday lives.

Rather than bringing in someone to provide full-time care, the Kelces turn to their extended family for additional support.

Kelce said maintaining those close family relationships is particularly important because she wants her daughters to grow up with the same values that shaped her and Jason's childhoods.

She also emphasized how grateful she is for the nanny who helps the family during the workday, acknowledging that the additional support is essential to managing their responsibilities.

However, the couple's approach to childcare goes beyond convenience.

For Kelce, living close to her parents has been a priority, and she admitted that the thought of settling somewhere far from her family has never appealed to her.

“We couldn’t do it without my parents. My parents live 15 minutes from us, and they have helped us in every way imaginable," she said.

The Pennsylvania native explained that remaining in the area where she grew up has allowed her to preserve those relationships while raising her own family.

That connection to her hometown has also influenced the kind of upbringing she hopes to provide her daughters.

Kelce wants the girls to become familiar with the places that shaped her childhood, from local businesses to the community organizations that continue to play a role in her life.

She believes that growing up with those personal connections can help children develop a stronger sense of responsibility toward the communities around them.

Her own involvement with the Eagles Autism Foundation is one example of how she has maintained those local ties.

For Kelce, the opportunity to contribute to the community where she was raised is another benefit of remaining close to home.

“Now, granted, we do have a nanny. I always give a shoutout because we couldn’t do it without her, but we have her during regular work hours and really nothing outside of that," she added.

Kelsi Karruli is a senior celebrity reporter who joined Realtor.com in 2025. She writes about trends encompassing real estate and celebrity properties. She previously wrote for Daily Mail, True Urban Culture, and JMedia Corporation, where she specialized in covering celebrities, relationships, psychology, and gender issues. She has a bachelor's degree in journalism from Pace University, where she received the James Rose Award for excellence in journalism. When she isn’t updating readers on A-listers' moves, she is spending time with her cat, Mary, and cheering on the Albanian soccer team.

Viktor Gyökeres the difference as Arsenal claim nervy win against Slavia Prague

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Another away day, another victory for Arsenal. This time, the much-maligned Viktor Gyökeres stole the show as poor Slavia Prague matched the longest winless streak in European Cup and Champions League history despite a spirited late comeback. That now stands at 23, equalling the mark set by AEK Athens and FCSB – formerly known as Steaua Bucharest – while Arsenal are now unbeaten in their last 10 away European trips. After last week’s wobble against Fleetwood, there was no prospect of Arsenal making the same mistake again as soon as Noni Madueke fired them ahead early on after being superbly set up by Gyökeres.

That was his third assist in his last five appearances, although Mikel Arteta will have been even more encouraged by the emphatic finish that had seemed to put the game beyond Slavia’s reach just before the break. While Kai Havertz remains his undoubted first choice, having another striker in form will only boost Arsenal’s challenge and it was noticeable that there was an extra spring in the 28-year-old’s step after his goal.

Slavia’s solitary victory in this competition came in their first-ever match in 2007 and, after losing 3-0 to Arsenal here exactly a year ago, the Czech champions knew their chances of avoiding the unwanted milestone were extremely slim. While Fleetwood’s giant-killing against the Premier League champions in the EFL Cup last week may have given them some hope of an upset, Arteta’s side has developed a killer instinct on their travels in Europe. Not since May 2025, in the second leg of their semi-final against Paris Saint-Germain, have Arsenal lost an away game. The 2-2 draw at Bayern Munich two weeks ago underlined their credentials as genuine contenders once again.

After their exploits at Anfield on Sunday that took them three points clear at the top of the Premier League, Arteta heavily rotated his side again with six changes. Martín Zubimendi was handed only his third start of a frustrating campaign for the Spain World Cup winner, while Gyökeres led the line for the third time in Arsenal’s past four matches.

The Sweden striker finished as top scorer last season with 21 goals and finally got off the mark against Fleetwood with a scrappy finish, although he has clearly been struggling for form and confidence. Yet there was no doubting the quality of his assist for Madueke’s goal after just eight minutes. David Raya’s long ball should have been dealt with by the Slavia defence but they made the criminal error of allowing it to bounce. That was the cue for Gyökeres to come alive as he beat his marker to the ball, chested it down and played the perfect pass for the England forward to run on to and score. Arteta’s reaction on the touchline suggested he enjoyed it too.

Mikel Merino was Arsenal’s hero here last year with two goals and he could have extended their lead on three occasions before the half-hour mark. The lively Madueke created his best opportunity after cutting the ball back to Merino but he could not beat Nazar Domchak in Slavia’s goal.

With Martin Ødegaard watching from the bench next to William Saliba, on his return from a long-term back injury, Arsenal lacked some of their usual fluency in attack. But Slavia were still no match for them and their task became almost impossible seven minutes before the break. Gyökeres exchanged a smart one-two with Merino inside the area before curling a brilliant shot into the far corner. It needed a goalline clearance from Michal Sadilek to prevent a rampant Arsenal inflicting more damage before half-time.

To their credit, Slavia started the second half with much more aggression after introducing their record signing Ange N’Guessan from the bench. The hosts came within a few centimetres of being awarded a penalty when Emmanuel Ayaosi was brought down by Ezri Konsa on the edge of the area. The England defender and Rice both found themselves in the book as Arsenal struggled to recapture their rhythm from the first half without ever looking in danger of conceding.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

But that all changed in the last 20 minutes after David Jurasek let fly from long range and his effort was deflected just wide of Raya’s goal. From the resulting corner, Gabriel Magalhães somehow managed to clear two goalbound headers off the line in a matter of seconds. The Brazilian celebrated with a massive fist pump.

Mikulas Konecny should have done much better with another headed chance as Arsenal’s formidable defence was put to the test in the final 15 minutes. A spectacular save denied Oskar Kubiak as Raya managed to push the ball on to the post at full stretch. Slavia eventually scored the goal all their endeavour deserved in the final minute of normal time when Wiktor Nowak fired in at the far post. There were a few nervous moments in stoppage time and Arteta breathed a sigh of relief at the full-time whistle as Slavia were condemned to their fate.
